ROW 6 | City of Los Angeles → City of Beverly Hills
Expected: contains
==========================================================================================

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──
A: City of Los Angeles | B: City of Beverly Hills | Vernacular: "A massive city polygon completely enveloping an independent smaller city polygon."

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
"""

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 3. RAG REASONER (Geospatial Topology) – Handles the Geospatial Reasoning
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #

class GeoTopologyRagReasoner:
    """
    Handles geospatial topological reasoning using the OSM RAG system.
    """

    def __init__(self, rag_system: OSMRagSystem):
        self.rag_system = rag_system

    def reason(self, entity_a: OSMEntity, entity_b: OSMEntity, vernacular: str) -> str:
        """
        Performs geospatial topological reasoning between two entities.
        """
        # 1. Validate entities against the context
        if not self._validate_entities(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular):
            return "Answer: disjoint"

        # 2. Build the prompt with the entity details and vernacular
        prompt = self._build_prompt(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)

        # 3. Query the RAG system
        response = self.rag_system.query(prompt)

        # 4. Parse the response to extract the suggested predicate
        predicate = self._extract_predicate(response)

        # 5. Return the final answer
        return f"Answer: {predicate}"

    def _validate_entities(self, entity_a: OSMEntity, entity_b: OSMEntity, vernacular: str) -> bool:
        """
        Checks if the entities are valid for topological reasoning.
        """
        # Entity type check
        if entity_a.type != entity_b.type:
            return False

        # Geometry type check
        if entity_a.geometry.type != entity_b.geometry.type:
            return False

        # Spatial relevance check
        if not self._spatially_relevant(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular):
            return False

        return True

    def _spatially_relevant(self, entity_a: OSMEntity, entity_b: OSMEntity, vernacular: str) -> bool:
        """
        Determines if the entities are spatially relevant based on the vernacular.
        """
        # Use the spatial relevance function defined earlier
        return spatial_relevance(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)

    def _build_prompt(self, entity_a: OSMEntity, entity_b: OSMEntity, vernacular: str) -> str:
        """
        Constructs the reasoning prompt for the LLM.
        """
        # Format the entity details
        entity_a_str = f"Entity A: {entity_a.name} (type: {entity_a.type}, geometry: {entity_a.geometry.type})"
        entity_b_str = f"Entity B: {entity_b.name} (type: {entity_b.type}, geometry: {entity_b.geometry.type})"

        # Combine with the vernacular
        prompt = f"{entity_a_str}\n{entity_b_str}\nVernacular: \"{vernacular}\""

        # Append the reasoning template
        prompt += "\n\n" + RAG_REASONING_TEMPLATE

        return prompt

    def _extract_predicate(self, response: str) -> str:
        """
        Parses the LLM response to find the suggested predicate.
        """
        # Look for the line starting with "Suggested predicate:"
        for line in response.splitlines():
            if line.lower().startswith("suggested predicate:"):
                return line.split(":", 1)[1].strip()
        # Fallback: look for the final "Answer:" line
        for line in response.splitlines():
            if line.lower().startswith("answer:"):
                return line.split(":", 1)[1].strip()
        # Default if nothing found
        return "disjoint"

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 4. MAIN REASONER – Orchestrates the Entire Process
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #

class GeoSpatialReasoner:
    """
    Main orchestrator for geospatial topological reasoning.
    """

    def __init__(self, rag_system: OSMRagSystem):
        self.rag_system = rag_system
        self.geo_reasoner = GeoTopologyRagReasoner(rag_system)

    def reason(self, entity_a: OSMEntity, entity_b: OSMEntity, vernacular: str) -> str:
        """
        Performs the full reasoning workflow between two entities.
        """
        # 1. Validate entities
        if not self._validate_entities(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular):
            return "Answer: disjoint"

        # 2. Perform geospatial topological reasoning
        return self.geo_reasoner.reason(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)

    def _validate_entities(self, entity_a: OSMEntity, entity_b: OSMEntity, vernacular: str) -> bool:
        """
        Checks if the entities are valid for topological reasoning.
        """
        # Entity type check
        if entity_a.type != entity_b.type:
            return False

        # Geometry type check
        if entity_a.geometry.type

  [ToT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: contains

              "exact same extent as", "identical perimeter", "perfect mapping symmetry"
              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, and exterior)

WARNING — ambiguous vernacular (read carefully):
  "county seat of" → does NOT imply within — the city may extend into adjacent counties → overlaps
  "suburb of"      → does NOT imply touches — suburb may be separated from the main city → disjoint
  "surrounded by"  → may be touches (partial encirclement), not within (full containment)
  "enclave of"     → touches (shares boundary, not interior containment)
  "along" + river/road → likely crosses (full LineString traversal), not just touches
  "same as" / "mirrors" → may be equals only if BOTH geometries are truly identical extents


Rules:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
   — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?

ROW 14 | Republic of Italy → San Marino

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
"""

REASONING_PROMPT = """
You are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning.

Vernacular-to-Topology Reference:
  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)
  CONTAINS  — "is home to", "includes", "hosts"             (A fully encloses B)
  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of",
              "surrounded by", "across from" (water boundary)
  CROSSES   — "passes through", "along", "traverses",
              "at the mouth of", "upstream from" (full LineString)
  OVERLAPS  — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles",
              "part of population in", "county seat of" (may extend into adjacent polygons)
  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from" (confirmed large distance)
  EQUALS    — "is identical to", "same boundary as", "mirrors exactly",
              "exact same extent as", "identical perimeter", "perfect mapping symmetry"
              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, exterior)

Rules:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
   — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
   — If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "most sides", "surrounded by" with incomplete encirclement → touches.
   — "partly in", "extends into", "part of population in" → overlaps.
   — "enclave of" → touches (shared boundary, not interior containment).
   — "across from" over water → touches (water boundary is shared in GIS).
   — "county seat of" alone does NOT imply within.

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — LineString (river, road) traversing a Polygon interior → crosses.
   — LineString touching only Polygon boundary → touches.
   — "At the mouth of" / "upstream from": verify if the full LineString crosses the polygon.
   — Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps.

4. BOUNDING BOX CAUTION (never conclude directly from bbox):
   — bbox overlap ≠ polygon overlap. Polygons inside overlapping bboxes may still be disjoint.
   — bbox containment ≠ within/contains. A country bbox contains almost all nearby entities.
   — Treat bbox as a clue, never as proof.

5. BBOX GAP TOLERANCE:
   — A gap of < 0.01 degrees (~1 km) between bboxes is NOT proof of disjoint.
   — Administrative boundaries often share exact edges with 0-meter gap.

6. ENTITY VALIDATION:
   — Verify the retrieved OSM entity matches the expected state/region.
   — If the entity location does not match the context, treat its geometry as unreliable.

7. FULL LINESTRING REASONING:
   — For rivers and roads, reason on the entire geometry, not just the centroid/representative point.
   — A river centroid may fall outside a city even if the river traverses it.

8. EQUALS CHECK (do before all other predicates):
   — equals requires BOTH entities to have the IDENTICAL geometry (exact same interior, boundary, exterior).
   — Strong equals signals: "identical perimeter", "same boundary", "coextensive",
     "municipal limits mirror the county boundary", "perfect mapping symmetry".
   — Do NOT use equals unless the text explicitly states geometric identity.

9. PICK EXACTLY ONE predicate from: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.
10. END WITH: Answer: [predicate]

CRITICAL DISTINCTIONS (most confused predicates):

equals vs all others (CHECK THIS FIRST):
  equals   → A and B have the IDENTICAL geometry. Every point of A is a point of B and vice versa.
             Bbox sizes will be nearly identical; centroids will coincide.
             Strong signals: "same boundary", "identical perimeter", "coextensive",
             "municipal limits mirror the county boundary", "perfect mapping symmetry".
  contains / within → different size entities — one fully inside the other, NOT identical.
  overlaps → partial overlap — NEVER equals.
  If in doubt: equals is rare. Do not use it unless geometric identity is explicitly stated.

touches vs overlaps:
  touches  → A and B share ONLY their boundary. Interiors do NOT intersect.
             Example: two adjacent counties sharing a border line.
  overlaps → A and B share SOME interior area. Part of A is inside B

  [ToT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: contains

RESULT | Expected=contains | Predicted=contains | CORRECT | Acc=53.33%

==========================================================================================
ROW 15 | City of Paris → France
Expected: within
==========================================================================================

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──
A: City of Paris | B: France | Vernacular: "A city polygon entirely trapped by a nation polygon."

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
"""

def get_geospatial_reasoning(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular):
    # 1. Retrieve OSM entities
    entity_a_osm = get_osm_entity(entity_a)
    entity_b_osm = get_osm_entity(entity_b)

    # 2. Build prompt
    prompt = VERNACULAR_TO_TOPOLOGY_PROMPT.format(
        entity_a=entity_a_osm,
        entity_b=entity_b_osm,
        vernacular=vernacular
    )

    # 3. Query LLM
    response = query_llm(prompt)

    # 4. Parse response
    branches = parse_branches(response)

    # 5. Return structured reasoning
    return {
        "entity_a": entity_a_osm,
        "entity_b": entity_b_osm,
        "vernacular": vernacular,
        "branches": branches
    }

# Example usage
entity_a = "City of Paris"
entity_b = "France"
vernacular = "City of Paris A city polygon entirely trapped by a nation polygon. France"

reasoning = get_geospatial_reasoning(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)
print(reasoning)
```

```json
{
  "entity_a": {
    "name": "City of Paris",
    "type": "City of Paris",
    "geometry": "Polygon"
  },
  "entity_b": {
    "name": "France",
    "type": "France",
    "geometry": "Polygon"
  },
  "vernacular": "City of Paris A city polygon entirely trapped by a nation polygon. France",
  "branches": [
    {
      "id": 1,
      "approach": "EQUALS CHECK",
      "reasoning": "The text explicitly states 'identical perimeter' and 'coextensive'. Both entities are polygons and share the same bounding box. No contradictory qualifiers are present. The OSM data confirms that the city limits exactly match the national boundary.",
      "predicate": "equals"
    },
    {
      "id": 2,
      "approach": "WITHIN REASONING",
      "reasoning": "The phrase 'entirely trapped by' indicates that the city polygon is fully inside the country polygon. The OSM geometry for Paris is smaller than France's geometry. No textual evidence suggests the city extends beyond the nation's borders.",
      "predicate": "within"
    },
    {
      "id": 3,
      "approach": "DISJOINT CHECK",
      "reasoning": "The description 'entirely trapped' contradicts any notion of separation. The bbox overlap is complete and the polygon containment is clear. No textual qualifier indicates distance or separation.",
      "predicate": "disjoint"
    }
  ]
}
```

**Key Points**:
- The LLM is explicitly instructed to produce three distinct reasoning branches.
- Each branch must follow the hierarchy: equals → within/contains → touches/crosses/overlaps → disjoint.
- The prompt emphasizes the importance of directionality and textual qualifiers over geometric heuristics.
- The final answer is a structured JSON with three suggested predicates, each backed by a short reasoning snippet.

  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
Entity A: Badlands National Park (type: Badlands National Park, geometry: Polygon)
Entity B: State of South Dakota (type: State of South Dakota, geometry: Polygon)
Vernacular: "Badlands National Park A park trapped by a complete geometric host. State of South Dakota" 
OSM Evidence: 
  A: Badlands National Park (Polygon)
  B: State of South Dakota (Polygon)



You are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning.

Vernacular-to-Topology Reference:
  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)
  CONTAINS  — "is home to", "includes", "hosts"             (A fully encloses B)
  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of",
              "surrounded by", "across from" (water boundary)
  CROSSES   — "passes through", "along", "traverses",
              "at the mouth of" / "upstream from" (full LineString)
  OVERLAPS  — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles",
              "part of population in", "county seat of" (may overlap adjacent polygons)
  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from" (large distance)
  EQUALS    — "is identical to", "same boundary as", "mirrors exactly",
              "exact same extent as", "identical perimeter", "perfect mapping symmetry"
              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, and exterior)

Rules:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
   — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
   — If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles", "county seat of" → overlaps.
   — "enclave of", "surrounded by" → touches (shared boundary, not interior containment).
   — "crosses" with rivers/roads → crosses (LineString traverses Polygon interior).
   — "disjoint" with large distance → disjoint.

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — Polygon vs Polygon: within, contains, touches, overlaps, disjoint, equals.
   — LineString vs Polygon: crosses (entire line passes through polygon), touches (only boundary contact).

4. BOUNDING BOX RULES:
   — Do NOT answer based solely on bbox overlap or containment.
   — Use bbox only as a hint; rely on actual geometry for final predicate.

5. EQUALS CHECK FIRST:
   — If the text explicitly states geometric identity (e.g., "same boundary as"), use equals.
   — Otherwise, proceed with other predicates.

6. DISJOINT CONFIRMATION:
   — If the vernacular mentions a large distance (e.g., "miles away") AND the bbox distance is significant, use disjoint.

7. WITHIN vs CONTAINS:
   — Determine which entity is the larger geometric host.
   — If B encloses A → within (A inside B).
   — If A encloses B → contains (A surrounds B).

8. OVERLAPS vs TOUCHES:
   — Overlaps: shared interior area.
   — Touches: shared boundary only.

9. FINAL RULES:
   — Pick EXACTLY ONE predicate from: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.
   — End with: Suggested predicate: [predicate]   (use the same format as in the examples)



CRITICAL DISTINCTIONS (most confused predicates):

equals vs all others (CHECK THIS FIRST):
  equals   → A and B have the IDENTICAL geometry. Bbox sizes will be nearly identical; centroids will coincide.
             Strong signals: "same boundary", "identical perimeter", "coextensive",
             "municipal limits mirror the county boundary", "perfect mapping symmetry".
  contains / within → different size entities — one fully inside the other, NOT identical.
  overlaps → partial interior sharing — NOT equals.
  If in doubt: equals is rare. Do NOT use it unless geometric identity is explicitly stated.

touches vs overlaps:
  touches  → A and B share ONLY their boundary. Interiors do NOT intersect.
             Example: two adjacent counties sharing a border line.
  overlaps → A and B share SOME interior area. Part of A is inside B, part is outside.
             Example: a city that partially crosses a county boundary.

contains vs within (DIRECTION IS CRITICAL):
  contains → A is the CONTAINER. B is fully INSIDE A.

"""
    # Use the OpenAI API to generate the reasoning
    response = openai.ChatCompletion.create(
        model="gpt-4o-mini",
        messages=[
            {"role": "system", "content": system_prompt},
            {"role": "user", "content": user_prompt}
        ],
        temperature=0.0,
        max_tokens=500
    )
    reasoning_text = response['choices'][0]['message']['content']
    # Extract the final answer from the reasoning text
    final_answer = extract_final_answer(reasoning_text)
    return reasoning_text, final_answer

def extract_final_answer(text):
    """
    Extracts the final predicate answer from the reasoning text.
    """
    # Look for the line that starts with "Answer:" and get the predicate
    match = re.search(r"Answer:\s*(contains|within|touches|crosses|disjoint|overlaps|equals)", text, re.IGNORECASE)
    if match:
        return match.group(1).lower()
    # Fallback: look for the last predicate in the text
    for predicate in ["contains", "within", "touches", "crosses", "disjoint", "overlaps", "equals"]:
        if predicate in text.lower():
            return predicate
    return "unknown"

def main():
    # Example usage
    entity_a = {"name": "Yellowstone Caldera", "type": "Yellowstone Caldera", "geometry": "Polygon"}
    entity_b = {"name": "Yellowstone National Park", "type": "Yellowstone National Park", "geometry": "Polygon"}
    vernacular = "Yellowstone Caldera A subterranean volcanic polygon perfectly bounded by an administrative surface polygon. Yellowstone National Park"

    reasoning, final_predicate = reason_with_osm(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)
    print("\n=== Reasoning Text ===")
    print(reasoning)
    print("\n=== Final Predicate ===")
    print(final_predicate)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

**Explanation of the Prompt Design**

1. **System Message**  
   - Sets the role of the assistant as an expert geospatial topological reasoner.  
   - Provides the exact list of valid predicates and the rule that only one may be chosen.  
   - Gives a concise reference table mapping common vernacular to the topological predicates.  
   - Emphasizes the importance of directionality (A→B vs B→A).  
   - Warns against over‑reliance on bounding boxes and highlights the need for textual qualifiers.

2. **User Message**  
   - Presents the two entities and the ambiguous vernacular.  
   - Explicitly asks the model to explore **three distinct reasoning branches**.  
   - Each branch must follow the same structure: approach, reasoning, suggested predicate.  
   - The prompt forces the model to consider multiple possibilities and to weigh textual evidence against geometric signals.

3. **OpenAI API Call**  
   - Uses a deterministic temperature (0.0) to ensure reproducible reasoning.  
   - Limits the token count to keep the response concise.

4. **Post‑Processing**  
   - The `extract_final_answer` helper pulls the predicate from the model’s output.  
   - The `main` function demonstrates how to call the reasoning routine and display both the reasoning text and the chosen predicate.

**Why This Prompt Works**

- **Explicit Instruction** – The model is told exactly what to do: reason with OSM, produce three branches, pick one predicate.
- **Geometric Prioritization** – The prompt reminds the model that polygon geometry is reliable, while bbox overlap is only a hint.
- **Textual Weighting** – By listing strong textual signals, the model learns to favor them over ambiguous spatial cues.
- **Directionality Emphasis** – The rule “Ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?” forces the model to resolve the core ambiguity before selecting a predicate.
- **Controlled Output** – The structured format makes it easy to parse the model’s answer programmatically.

This prompt is designed to be robust against the common pitfalls of geospatial reasoning with LLMs, ensuring that the final predicate is chosen based on the best available evidence.

**Key Points to Remember**:
- **Always ask for direction** before reasoning. The user may list entities in any order.
- **Validate the OSM entity** against the expected state or region. If it mismatches, treat its geometry as unreliable.
- **Prioritize textual qualifiers** over geometric cues. Vague language like “near” or “close to” is insufficient.
- **Never rely solely on bounding boxes**. Use them as hints, not proof.
- **Follow the 4-step disambiguation** strictly. The final predicate must come from that ordered list.
- **End with the exact format**: “Suggested predicate: [predicate]”.

Good luck!

ROW 26 | Diego Garcia → Indian Ocean
Expected: within
==========================================================================================

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──
A: Diego Garcia | B: Indian Ocean | Vernacular: "A highly restricted atoll perfectly isolated by an absolute oceanic perimeter."

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
Answer: touches



We are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning.

Vernacular-to-Topology Reference:
  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)
  CONTAINS  — "is home to", "includes", "hosts"             (A fully encloses B)
  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of",
              "surrounded by", "across from" (water boundary)
  CROSSES   — "passes through", "along", "traverses",
              "at the mouth of" / "upstream from" (full LineString)
  OVERLAPS  — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles",
              "part of population in", "county seat of" (may extend into adjacent counties)
  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from" (confirmed large distance)
  EQUALS    — "is identical to", "same boundary as", "mirrors exactly",
              "exact same extent as", "identical perimeter", "perfect mapping symmetry"
              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, and exterior)

Rules:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
   — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
   — If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "most sides", "surrounded by" with incomplete encirclement → touches.
   — "partly in", "extends into", "part of population in" → overlaps.
   — "enclave of" → touches (shared boundary, not interior containment).
   — "across from" over water → touches (water boundary is shared in GIS).
   — "county seat of" alone does NOT imply within.

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — LineString (river, road) traversing a Polygon interior → crosses.
   — LineString touching only Polygon boundary → touches.
   — "At the mouth of" / "upstream from": verify if the full LineString crosses the polygon.
   — Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps.

4. BOUNDING BOX CAUTION (never conclude directly from bbox):
   — bbox overlap ≠ polygon overlap. Polygons inside overlapping bboxes may still be disjoint.
   — bbox containment ≠ within/contains. A country bbox contains almost all nearby entities.
   — Treat bbox as a clue, never as proof.

5. BBOX GAP TOLERANCE:
   — A gap of < 0.01 degrees (~1 km) between bboxes is NOT proof of disjoint.
   — Administrative boundaries often share exact edges with 0-meter gap.

6. ENTITY VALIDATION:
   — Verify the retrieved OSM entity matches the expected state/region.
   — If the entity location does not match the context, treat its geometry as unreliable.

7. FULL LINESTRING REASONING:
   — For rivers and roads, reason on the entire geometry, not just the centroid/representative point.
   — A river centroid may fall outside a city even if the river traverses it.

8. EQUALS CHECK (do before all other predicates):
   — equals requires BOTH entities to have the IDENTICAL geometry (exact same interior, boundary, exterior).
   — Strong equals signals: "identical perimeter", "same boundary", "coextensive", "municipal limits mirror the county boundary", "perfect mapping symmetry".
   — Do NOT use equals unless the text explicitly states geometric identity.

9. PICK EXACTLY ONE predicate from: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.
10. END WITH: Answer: [predicate]



CRITICAL DISTINCTIONS (most confused predicates):

equals vs all others (CHECK THIS FIRST):
  equals   → A and B have the IDENTICAL geometry. Every point of A is a point of B and vice versa.
             Bbox sizes will be nearly identical; centroids will coincide.
             Strong signals: "same boundary", "identical perimeter", "coextensive", "municipal limits mirror the county boundary", "perfect mapping symmetry".
  contains / within → different size entities — one fully inside the other, NOT identical.
  overlaps → partial overlap — NEVER equals.
  If in doubt: equals is rare. Do not use it unless geometric identity is explicitly stated.

touches vs overlaps:
  touches  → A and B share ONLY their boundary. Interiors do NOT intersect.
             Example: two adjacent counties sharing a border line.
  overlaps → A and B share SOME interior area. Part of A is inside B, part is

def answer_question(question: str) -> str:
    prompt = f"{BASE_PROMPT}\n\nQuestion: {question}\n\nAnswer:"
    response = openai.ChatCompletion.create(
        model="gpt-4o-mini",
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
        temperature=0.0,
        max_tokens=2000,
        stop=None,
    )
    return response.choices[0].message.content.strip()

def main():
    question = "Vernacular: 'Brazil Two South American polygons sharing a border edge. Argentina'"
    answer = answer_question(question)
    print(answer)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

REASONING_PROMPT = """
You are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning.

Vernacular-to-Topology Reference:
  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)
  CONTAINS  — "is home to", "includes", "hosts"             (A fully encloses B)
  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of",
              "surrounded by", "across from" (water boundary)
  CROSSES   — "passes through", "along", "traverses",
              "at the mouth of" / "upstream from" (full LineString)
  OVERLAPS  — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles",
              "part of population in", "county seat of" (may extend into adjacent counties)
  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from" (confirmed large distance)
  EQUALS    — "is identical to", "same boundary as", "mirrors exactly",
              "exact same extent as", "identical perimeter", "perfect mapping symmetry"
              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, and exterior)

Rules for Reasoning:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
   — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
   — If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "partly in", "extends into", "part of population in" → overlaps.
   — "enclave of", "surrounded by" with incomplete encirclement → touches.
   — "crosses" with a full LineString entity → crosses.
   — "equals" with identical geometry description → equals.
   — "disjoint" with large distance description → disjoint.

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — LineString (river, road) traversing a Polygon interior → crosses.
   — LineString touching only Polygon boundary → touches.
   — Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps.

4. BOUNDING BOX CAUTION (never conclude directly from bbox):
   — bbox overlap ≠ polygon overlap. Polygons inside overlapping bboxes may still be disjoint.
   — bbox containment ≠ within/contains. A country bbox contains almost all nearby entities.
   — Treat bbox as a clue, never as proof.

5. BBOX GAP TOLERANCE:
   — A gap of < 0.01 degrees (~1 km) between bboxes is NOT proof of disjoint.
   — Administrative boundaries often share exact edges with 0-meter gap.

6. ENTITY VALIDATION:
   — Verify the retrieved OSM entity matches the expected state/region.
   — If the entity location does not match the context, treat its geometry as unreliable.

7. FULL LINESTRING REASONING:
   — For rivers and roads, reason on the entire geometry, not just the centroid/representative point.
   — A river centroid may fall outside a city even if the river traverses it.

8. EQUALS CHECK (do before all other predicates):
   — equals requires BOTH entities to have the IDENTICAL geometry (exact same interior, boundary, exterior).
   — Strong equals signals: "identical perimeter", "same boundary", "mirrors exactly",
     "exact same extent as", "perfect mapping symmetry", "coextensive".
   — Do NOT use equals unless the text explicitly states geometric identity.

9. PICK EXACTLY ONE predicate from: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.
10. END WITH: Suggested predicate: [predicate]



CRITICAL DISTINCTIONS (most confused predicates):

equals vs all others (most rare):
  equals   → A and B have the IDENTICAL geometry. Every point of A is a point of B and vice versa.
             Bbox sizes will be nearly identical; centroids will coincide.
             Strong signals: "identical perimeter", "same boundary", "mirrors exactly",
             "exact same extent as", "perfect mapping symmetry", "coextensive".
  If in doubt: equals is rare. Do NOT use it unless geometric identity is explicitly stated.

touches vs overlaps:
  touches  → A and B share ONLY their boundary. Interiors do NOT intersect.
             Example: two adjacent counties sharing a border line.
  overlaps → A and B share SOME interior area. Part of A is inside B, part is outside.
             Example: a city that partially crosses a county boundary.

touches vs crosses:
  touches  → boundary contact only, no traversal through interior.
  crosses  → A passes THROUGH B, entering AND exiting its

  [ToT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: touches

RESULT | Expected=touches | Predicted=touches | CORRECT | Acc=57.14%

==========================================================================================
ROW 40 | State of Colorado → State of New Mexico
Expected: touches
==========================================================================================

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──
A: State of Colorado | B: State of New Mexico | Vernacular: "Two state administrative polygons meeting at a geometric edge."

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
"""

def get_geospatial_reasoning(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular):
    # Retrieve OSM entities for A and B
    entity_a_osm = get_osm_entity(entity_a)
    entity_b_osm = get_osm_entity(entity_b)
    
    # Build the prompt with the retrieved entities
    prompt = prompt_template.format(
        entity_a=entity_a_osm,
        entity_b=entity_b_osm,
        vernacular=vernacular
    )
    
    # Call the LLM to reason geospatially
    response = llm_call(prompt)
    
    # Parse the LLM's suggested predicate
    predicate = parse_predicate(response)
    
    # Return the reasoning and the chosen predicate
    return response, predicate

# Example usage
entity_a = "State of Colorado"
entity_b = "State of New Mexico"
vernacular = "State of Colorado Two state administrative polygons meeting at a geometric edge. State of New Mexico"

reasoning, predicate = get_geospatial_reasoning(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)
print(reasoning)
print("Answer:", predicate)
```

**Explanation of the Prompt Design**

1. **Explicit Entity Retrieval**  
   The function `get_osm_entity` is a placeholder for a geospatial lookup service that returns the OSM entity name, type, and geometry. By embedding these entities directly into the prompt, we give the LLM concrete, verifiable data to reason with.

2. **Geospatial Predicate Hierarchy**  
   The prompt lists the valid predicates in the exact order the reasoning rules dictate. This forces the LLM to follow the 4‑step disambiguation hierarchy: equals → within → contains → overlaps → touches → crosses → disjoint.

3. **Vernacular‑to‑Topology Mapping**  
   The prompt explicitly states the mapping between common spatial descriptors and the topological predicates. This removes ambiguity and aligns the LLM’s internal knowledge with the reasoning framework.

4. **Rule‑Based Reasoning**  
   The prompt enumerates the reasoning rules in a bullet‑point format. By presenting the rules as a checklist, we guide the LLM to systematically evaluate the spatial relationship.

5. **Entity Validation Warning**  
   The prompt includes a “WARNING – ambiguous vernacular” section that lists tricky phrases and their correct topological interpretation. This pre‑empts the LLM’s tendency to misread “county seat of” as “within”.

6. **Geometric Constraints**  
   The rules explicitly mention geometry types (Polygon vs LineString) and how they influence the predicate (crosses vs touches). This ensures the LLM does not rely solely on textual cues when the geometry type is decisive.

7. **Bounding Box Caution**  
   The prompt warns against using bbox overlap as proof of spatial relation. This is critical because many administrative entities share the same bounding box but are disjoint.

8. **Three‑Branch Exploration**  
   The function asks the LLM to reason in three distinct ways, each time prioritizing different cues (textual vs geometric). This mirrors the human reasoning process and provides multiple evidence paths for the final decision.

9. **Explicit Direction Check**  
   The rules require the LLM to confirm whether the predicate applies A→B or B→A. This is essential for entities where the spatial relationship is not symmetric.

10. **Final Predicate Extraction**  
    The LLM is instructed to end with “Suggested predicate: [predicate]”. The wrapper function then parses this line to enforce the single‑predicate rule.

By structuring the prompt in this way, we give the LLM a clear, rule‑based framework to reason geospatially, ensuring that the final answer is consistent with the evidence and the topological hierarchy.

  [ToT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: touches

RESULT | Expected=touches | Predicted=touches | CORRECT | Acc=56.10%

ROW 43 | Four Corners Monument → State of Utah
Expected: touches
==========================================================================================

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──
A: Four Corners Monument | B: State of Utah | Vernacular: "A zero-dimensional node converging exactly at the extreme vertex of a massive state polygon."

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
"""

REASONING_PROMPT = """
You are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning.

Vernacular-to-Topology Reference:
  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)
  CONTAINS  — "is home to", "includes", "hosts"             (A fully encloses B)
  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of",
              "surrounded by", "across from" (water boundary)
  CROSSES   — "passes through", "along", "traverses",
              "at the mouth of" / "upstream from" (full LineString)
  OVERLAPS  — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles",
              "part of population in", "county seat of" (may extend into adjacent counties)
  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from" (confirmed large distance)
  EQUALS    — "is identical to", "same boundary as", "mirrors exactly",
              "exact same extent as", "identical perimeter", "perfect mapping symmetry"
              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, and exterior)

Rules for Reasoning:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
   — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
   — If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "partly in", "extends into", "part of population in" → overlaps.
   — "enclave of", "surrounded by" → touches (shared boundary, not interior containment).
   — "along" a river/road → crosses (full LineString traversal).
   — "same as", "identical perimeter" → equals (exact geometry match).

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — Point vs Polygon → cannot be contains/within/crosses/overlaps. Only touches or disjoint.
   — LineString vs Polygon → crosses if LineString passes through interior; touches if only boundary contact.
   — Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps.

4. BOUNDING BOX CAUTION (never conclude directly from bbox):
   — bbox overlap ≠ polygon overlap. Polygons inside overlapping bboxes may still be disjoint.
   — bbox containment ≠ within/contains. A country bbox contains almost all nearby entities.
   — Treat bbox as a clue, never as proof.

5. BBOX GAP TOLERANCE:
   — A gap of < 0.01 degrees (~1 km) between bboxes is NOT proof of disjoint.
   — Administrative boundaries often share exact edges with 0-meter gap.

6. ENTITY VALIDATION:
   — Verify the retrieved OSM entity matches the expected state/region.
   — If the entity location does not match the context, treat its geometry as unreliable.

7. FULL LINESTRING REASONING:
   — For rivers and roads, reason on the entire geometry, not just the centroid/representative point.
   — A river centroid may fall outside a city even if the river traverses it.

8. EQUALS CHECK (do before all other predicates):
   — equals requires BOTH entities to have the IDENTICAL geometry (exact same interior, boundary, exterior).
   — Strong equals signals: "identical perimeter", "same boundary", "coextensive",
     "municipal limits mirror the county boundary", "perfect mapping symmetry".
   — Do NOT use equals unless the text explicitly states geometric identity.

9. Pick EXACTLY ONE predicate from: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.
10. End with: Suggested predicate: [predicate]

Entity A: Four Corners Monument (type: Four Corners Monument, geometry: Point)
Entity B: State of Utah (type: State of Utah, geometry: Polygon)
Vernacular: "Four Corners Monument A zero-dimensional node converging exactly at the extreme vertex of a massive state polygon. State of Utah"

Provide three distinct reasoning branches (A, B, C). Each branch must:
  - Check for EQUALS first: does the text describe geometrically identical / coextensive entities?
  - Check entity validation warnings before using geometry.
  - Prioritize textual qualifiers over bbox signals.
  - Explicitly verify the A→B direction of the predicate.
  - Note whether reasoning is based on polygon geometry (reliable) or bbox only (use with caution).

Format exactly as:
BRANCH 1: [approach]
[reasoning]
Suggested predicate: [predicate]

BRANCH 2: [approach]
[reasoning]
Suggested predicate: [predicate

  [ToT] ── BRANCH_1 ──
Predicted: None

  [ToT] ── BRANCH_2 ──
Predicted: None
[approach]
[reasoning]

  [ToT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: equals

You are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning.

Vernacular-to-Topology Reference:
  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)
  CONTAINS  — "is home to", "includes", "hosts"             (A fully encloses B)
  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of",
              "surrounded by", "across from" (water boundary)
  CROSSES   — "passes through", "along", "traverses",
              "at the mouth of" / "upstream from" (full LineString)
  OVERLAPS  — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles",
              "part of population in", "county seat of" (may extend into adjacent counties)
  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from" (confirmed large distance)
  EQUALS    — "is identical to", "same boundary as", "mirrors exactly",
              "exact same extent as", "identical perimeter", "perfect mapping symmetry"
              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, and exterior)

Rules for Reasoning:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "passes through" means A traverses B → crosses.
   — Ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles", "county seat of" → overlaps.
   — "enclave of", "surrounded by" → touches (shared boundary, not interior containment).
   — "along" a river/road → crosses (full LineString traversal).
   — "same as", "identical perimeter" → equals (only if geometry is truly identical).

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — LineString (railway) traversing a Polygon interior → crosses.
   — LineString touching only Polygon boundary → touches.
   — Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps.

4. BOUNDING BOX CAUTION (never conclude directly from bbox):
   — bbox overlap ≠ polygon overlap. Entities may still be disjoint.
   — bbox containment ≠ within/contains. A larger country bbox may contain many smaller entities.
   — Treat bbox as a clue, never as proof.

5. ENTITY VALIDATION:
   — Verify the OSM entity matches the expected state/region.
   — If the entity location does not match the context, treat its geometry as unreliable.

6. FULL LINESTRING REASONING:
   — For rivers and roads, reason on the entire geometry, not just the centroid/representative point.
   — A railway line may cross a mountain range polygon.

7. EQUALS CHECK (do before all other predicates):
   — equals requires BOTH entities to have the IDENTICAL geometry (coextensive).
   — Strong equals signals: "identical perimeter", "same boundary", "mirrors exactly",
     "perfect mapping symmetry", "coextensive", "municipal limits mirror the county boundary".
   — Do NOT use equals unless the text explicitly states geometric identity.

8. PICK EXACTLY ONE predicate from: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.

9. End with: Suggested predicate: [predicate]

Entity A: Trans-Siberian Railway (type: Trans-Siberian Railway, geometry: LineString)
Entity B: Ural Mountains (type: Ural Mountains, geometry: Polygon)
Vernacular: "Trans-Siberian Railway A railway line passes through both the exterior and interior of a mountain range polygon. Ural Mountains"

Reasoning branches (use the OSM evidence):
"""

ROW 49 | Channel Tunnel → English Channel

==========================================================================================

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──
A: Channel Tunnel | B: English Channel | Vernacular: "A transit line geometry traverses the spatial footprint of a maritime polygon."

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
"""

REASONING_PROMPT = """
You are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning.

Vernacular-to-Topology Reference:
  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)
  CONTAINS  — "is home to", "includes", "hosts"             (A fully encloses B)
  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of",
              "surrounded by", "across from" (water boundary)
  CROSSES   — "passes through", "along", "traverses",
              "at the mouth of" / "upstream from" (full LineString)
  OVERLAPS  — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles",
              "part of population in", "county seat of" (may extend into adjacent counties)
  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from" (confirmed large distance)
  EQUALS    — "is identical to", "same boundary as", "mirrors exactly",
              "exact same extent as", "identical perimeter", "perfect mapping symmetry"
              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, and exterior)

Rules for Reasoning:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
   — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
   — If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "partly in", "extends into", "county seat of" → overlaps.
   — "enclave of", "surrounded by" → touches (shared boundary, not interior containment).
   — "along" a river/road → crosses (full LineString traversal).
   — "same as", "identical perimeter" → equals (exact geometry match).

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — LineString traversing a Polygon interior → crosses.
   — LineString touching only Polygon boundary → touches.
   — Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps.

4. BOUNDING BOX CAUTION (never conclude directly from bbox):
   — bbox overlap ≠ polygon overlap. Polygons inside overlapping bboxes may still be disjoint.
   — bbox containment ≠ within/contains. A country bbox contains almost all nearby entities.
   — Treat bbox as a clue, never as proof.

5. BBOX GAP TOLERANCE:
   — A gap of < 0.01 degrees (~1 km) between bboxes is NOT proof of disjoint.
   — Administrative boundaries often share exact edges with 0-meter gap.

6. ENTITY VALIDATION:
   — Verify the retrieved OSM entity matches the expected state/region.
   — If the entity location does not match the context, treat its geometry as unreliable.

7. FULL LINESTRING REASONING:
   — For rivers and roads, reason on the entire geometry, not just the centroid/representative point.
   — A river centroid may fall outside a city even if the river traverses it.

8. EQUALS CHECK (do before all other predicates):
   — equals requires BOTH entities to have the IDENTICAL geometry (exact same interior, boundary, exterior).
   — Strong equals signals: "identical perimeter", "same boundary", "mirrors exactly",
     "coextensive", "municipal limits mirror the county boundary", "perfect mapping symmetry".
   — Do NOT use equals unless the text explicitly states geometric identity.

9. PICK EXACTLY ONE predicate from: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.
10. END WITH: Suggested predicate: [predicate]

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──
A: Colorado River | B: Grand Canyon National Park | Vernacular: "A natural river line entirely traversing a federal park polygon."

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
"""

def get_geospatial_reasoning_branch(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular):
    """
    Returns a formatted string with three distinct reasoning branches for the spatial relationship.
    Each branch follows the required structure and applies the geospatial topological rules.
    """
    # Build the prompt with the provided entities and vernacular
    prompt = PROMPT_TEMPLATE.format(
        entity_a=entity_a,
        entity_b=entity_b,
        vernacular=vernacular
    )
    
    # Call the LLM to generate the reasoning
    response = llm.generate(prompt)
    
    # Return the raw response (already formatted)
    return response

# Example usage
entity_a = "Colorado River (type: Colorado River, geometry: LineString)"
entity_b = "Grand Canyon National Park (type: Grand Canyon National Park, geometry: Polygon)"
vernacular = "Colorado River A natural river line entirely traversing a federal park polygon. Grand Canyon National Park"

reasoning_output = get_geospatial_reasoning_branch(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)
print(reasoning_output)
```

**Key Points to Follow:**

1. **Always start with a DIRECTION CHECK** – explicitly ask whether the predicate applies A→B or B→A.
2. **EQUALS FIRST** – look for strong textual signals of geometric identity. If found, answer "equals".
3. **Entity Validation** – confirm that the OSM entity matches the expected state/region. If not, treat its geometry as unreliable.
4. **Textual Qualifiers Over BBOX** – prioritize phrases like "partly in", "extends into", "same boundary" over any bounding box overlap.
5. **Reasoning Branches** – produce three distinct, well‑reasoned options, each following the same format.
6. **End with the Suggested Predicate** – exactly one of: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.

**Remember:** The final answer must be a single predicate from the list above. Do NOT provide any additional text beyond the formatted reasoning and the predicate.

ROW 55 | Trans-Alaska Pipeline → Arctic Circle

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──

Entity A: Trans-Alaska Pipeline (type: Trans-Alaska Pipeline, geometry: LineString)
Entity B: Arctic Circle (type: Arctic Circle, geometry: LineString)
Vernacular: "Trans-Alaska Pipeline An infrastructure line intersecting a global latitudinal line. Arctic Circle" 
Valid predicates: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals



We must reason geospatially. The entities are both LineString geometries. The question: "Trans-Alaska Pipeline An infrastructure line intersecting a global latitudinal line. Arctic Circle". So A is the pipeline, B is the Arctic Circle. The predicate must describe the relationship from A to B. The geometry type is LineString for both. They are not polygons. So the topological predicates that apply to two LineString geometries are touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals. But "overlaps" is for polygons sharing area; for lines it's not typical. For lines, the topological predicates are: intersects, touches, crosses, disjoint, equals. But the allowed predicates list includes contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals. For two lines, contains and within are not applicable because they require a polygon containing a point or line. So the valid predicates are touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals. But we must pick one from the list. The reasoning must be based on geometry. The OSM data likely shows the pipeline geometry crossing the Arctic Circle geometry. The text says "intersecting a global latitudinal line". That implies the pipeline geometry crosses the Arctic Circle geometry. So the predicate is "crosses". The direction is A→B. So the answer: crosses.

We must produce the final answer exactly: "Answer: crosses".assistantfinalBRANCH 1: [EQUALS CHECK]
[reasoning]
  - The text does NOT describe the pipeline and the Arctic Circle as having identical geometry.
  - No strong equals signals ("identical perimeter", "same boundary") are present.
  - The entities are distinct infrastructure lines, not coextensive boundaries.
Suggested predicate: disjoint

BRANCH 2: [GEOMETRY REASONING – LINESTRING CROSSING]
[reasoning]
  - Both A and B are LineString geometries.
  - The vernacular explicitly states that the pipeline "intersects" the Arctic Circle line.
  - In geospatial topology, two LineString geometries that share points but are not coincident are considered to "cross".
  - The direction is A→B (pipeline crosses Arctic Circle).
Suggested predicate: crosses

BRANCH 3: [TOUCHES CHECK]
[reasoning]

DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before reasoning):
  — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
  — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
  — If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".

TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
  — "most sides", "surrounded by" with incomplete encirclement → touches.
  — "partly in", "extends into", "part of population in" → overlaps.
  — "enclave of" → touches (shared boundary, not interior containment).
  — "across from" over water → touches (water boundary is shared in GIS).
  — "county seat of" alone does NOT imply within.

GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
  — LineString (river, road) traversing a Polygon interior → crosses.
  — LineString touching only Polygon boundary → touches.
  — "At the mouth of" / "upstream from": verify if the full LineString crosses the polygon.
  — Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps.

ENTITY VALIDATION:
  — Verify the retrieved OSM entity matches the expected state/region.
  — If the entity location does not match the context, treat its geometry as unreliable.

FULL LINESTRING REASONING:
  — For rivers and roads, reason on the entire geometry, not just the centroid/representative point.
  — A river centroid may fall outside a city even if the river traverses it.

EQUALS CHECK (do before all other predicates):
  — equals requires BOTH entities to have the IDENTICAL geometry (exact same interior, boundary, exterior).
  — Strong equals signals: "identical perimeter", "exact same extent", "perfect mapping symmetry",
     "municipal limits mirror the county boundary", "coextensive", "same footprint".
  — Do NOT use equals unless the text explicitly states geometric identity.

Pick EXACTLY ONE predicate from: contains, within, touches, crosses, disjoint, overlaps, equals.
Answer: touches
"""

### 3.2.2 Reasoning Branches

We construct **three distinct reasoning paths** for the same query, each exploring a different hypothesis:

| Branch | Hypothesis | Direction | Geometric Evidence | Textual Evidence | Final Predicate |
|--------|------------|-----------|--------------------|------------------|-----------------|
| 1 | Japan touches Brazil (shared boundary) | A→B | Bbox overlap, no containment | “separated by vast oceans” (boundary contact) | touches |
| 2 | Japan overlaps Brazil (partial area share) | A→B | Bbox overlap, no containment | “partly in” (shared interior) | overlaps |
| 3 | Japan disjoint Brazil (no shared points) | A→B | Bbox overlap, no containment | “vast oceans” (geographic separation) | disjoint |

Each branch follows the same **four-step disambiguation**:

1. **EQUALS Check** – No textual claim of geometric identity → not equals.
2. **Within Check** – Is A inside B? No containment → not within.
3. **Contains Check** – Is B inside A? No containment → not contains.
4. **Overlaps Check** – Do A and B share interior area? Textual hints → possible overlaps.
5. **Touches vs Disjoint** – Do A and B share boundary? Textual hints → possible touches or disjoint.

We then **prioritize textual qualifiers** over mere bbox overlap. The final predicate is chosen based on the strongest textual evidence.

### 3.2.3 Entity Validation

The OSM entities for Japan and Brazil are **valid** (correct country geometries). No mismatch warning is triggered. We rely on the **polygon geometry** for spatial reasoning, not the bounding boxes.

==========================================================================================
ROW 64 | Suez Canal → Panama Canal
Expected: disjoint
==========================================================================================

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──
A: Suez Canal | B: Panama Canal | Vernacular: "Two canal line geometries separated by thousands of miles."

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
"""

def disjoint_reasoning_branch(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular):
    # 1. EQUALS check
    if entity_a['type'] == entity_b['type'] and entity_a['geometry'] == entity_b['geometry']:
        return "equals"
    
    # 2. Entity validation
    if entity_a['bbox'] is None or entity_b['bbox'] is None:
        return "disjoint"
    
    # 3. Direction check
    direction = ask_direction(entity_a['name'], entity_b['name'], vernacular)
    
    # 4. Textual qualifier priority
    if any(term in vernacular.lower() for term in ["identical perimeter", "same boundary", "coextensive", "perfect mapping symmetry", "municipal limits mirror county lines"]):
        return "equals"
    if any(term in vernacular.lower() for term in ["within", "located in", "in"]):
        return "within"
    if any(term in vernacular.lower() for term in ["contains", "home to", "includes", "hosts"]):
        return "contains"
    if any(term in vernacular.lower() for term in ["touches", "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of", "surrounded by", "across from"]):
        return "touches"
    if any(term in vernacular.lower() for term in ["crosses", "passes through", "along", "traverses", "at the mouth of", "upstream from"]):
        return "crosses"
    if any(term in vernacular.lower() for term in ["overlaps", "partly in", "extends into", "straddles", "county seat of"]):
        return "overlaps"
    
    # 5. Geometry type constraints
    if entity_a['geometry'] == "LineString" and entity_b['geometry'] == "Polygon":
        return "crosses"
    if entity_a['geometry'] == "Polygon" and entity_b['geometry'] == "LineString":
        return "crosses"
    
    # 6. BBOX reasoning
    if not bbox_intersects(entity_a['bbox'], entity_b['bbox']):
        return "disjoint"
    
    # 7. Default to disjoint if no clear rule applies
    return "disjoint"

def explore_reasoning_branches(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular):
    branches = []
    for i in range(3):
        predicate = disjoint_reasoning_branch(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)
        branches.append(f"Suggested predicate: {predicate}")
    return branches

# Example usage
entity_a = {
    'name': 'Suez Canal',
    'type': 'Suez Canal',
    'geometry': 'LineString',
    'bbox': (31.0, 30.0, 32.0, 31.0)
}
entity_b = {
    'name': 'Panama Canal',
    'type': 'Panama Canal',
    'geometry': 'LineString',
    'bbox': (80.0, 8.0, 81.0, 9.0)

              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, and exterior)

Rules for Reasoning:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
   — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
   — If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles", "county seat of" → overlaps.
   — "enclave of", "surrounded by" → touches (shared boundary, not interior containment).
   — "crosses" with a LineString entity → crosses (entire geometry traverses polygon).
   — "disjoint" with clear distance → disjoint.
   — "equals" with identical geometry → equals.

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — LineString (river, road) traversing a Polygon interior → crosses.
   — LineString touching only Polygon boundary → touches.
   — Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps.

4. BOUNDING BOX CAUTION (never conclude directly from bbox):
   — bbox overlap ≠ polygon overlap. Polygons inside overlapping bboxes may still be disjoint.
   — bbox containment ≠ within/contains. A country bbox contains almost all nearby entities.
   — Treat bbox as a clue, never as proof.

5. ENTITY VALIDATION:
   — Verify the retrieved OSM entity matches the expected state/region.
   — If the entity location does not match the context, treat its geometry as unreliable.

6. FULL LINESTRING REASONING:
   — For rivers and roads, reason on the entire geometry, not just the centroid/representative point.
   — A river centroid may fall outside a city even if the river traverses it.

7. EQUALS CHECK (do before all other predicates):
   — equals requires BOTH entities to have the IDENTICAL geometry (exact same interior, boundary, exterior).
   — Strong equals signals: "identical perimeter", "same boundary", "mirrors exactly",
     "exact same extent as", "perfect mapping symmetry", "coextensive".
   — Do NOT use equals unless the text explicitly states geometric identity.

  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)

  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of",
              "surrounded by", "across from" (water boundary)

  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from" (confirmed large distance)

              "exact same extent", "identical perimeter", "perfect mapping symmetry"
              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, exterior)

WARNING — ambiguous vernacular (read carefully):
  "county seat of" → does NOT imply within — the city may extend into adjacent counties → overlaps
  "suburb of"      → does NOT imply touches — suburb may be separated from the main city → disjoint
  "surrounded by"  → may be touches (partial encirclement), not within (full containment)
  "enclave of"     → touches (shares boundary, not interior containment)
  "along" + river/road → likely crosses (full LineString traversal), not just touches
  "same as" / "mirrors" → may be equals only if BOTH geometries are truly identical extents


Rules:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
   — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
   — If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "most sides", "surrounded by" with incomplete encirclement → touches.
   — "partly in", "extends into", "part of population in" → overlaps.
   — "enclave of" → touches (shared boundary, not interior containment).
   — "across from" over water → touches (water boundary is shared in GIS).
   — "county seat of" alone does NOT imply within.

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — LineString (river, road) traversing a Polygon interior → crosses.
   — LineString touching only Polygon boundary → touches.
   — "At the mouth of" / "upstream from": verify if the full LineString crosses the polygon.
   — Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps.

4. BOUNDING BOX CAUTION (never conclude directly from bbox):
   — bbox overlap ≠ polygon overlap. Polygons inside overlapping bboxes may still be disjoint.
   — bbox containment ≠ within/contains. A country bbox contains almost all nearby entities.
   — Treat bbox as a clue, never as proof.

5. BBOX GAP TOLERANCE:
   — A gap of < 0.01 degrees (~1 km) between bboxes is NOT proof of disjoint.
   — Administrative boundaries often share exact edges with 0-meter gap.

6. ENTITY VALIDATION:
   — Verify the retrieved OSM entity matches the expected state/region.

- Suggested predicate: **overlaps**  

**Final Decision**  
All reasoning branches converge on the same topological relationship: partial spatial overlap between the Amazon Rainforest and Brazil.

Answer: overlaps

  [ToT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: overlaps

RESULT | Expected=overlaps | Predicted=overlaps | CORRECT | Acc=57.14%

==========================================================================================
ROW 77 | Sahara Desert → Egypt
Expected: overlaps
==========================================================================================

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──
A: Sahara Desert | B: Egypt | Vernacular: "An environmental polygon partially intersecting a national administrative polygon."

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
"""

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 5. Geospatial Reasoning Engine (Vernacular-to-Topology)
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #

class GeoReasoner:
    """
    Core engine that takes a pair of geospatial entities and a natural language
    description, then produces a topological predicate using the OSM evidence.
    """

    def __init__(self, openai_api_key: str):
        self.openai_api_key = openai_api_key
        self.client = OpenAI(api_key=openai_api_key)

    def _build_prompt(self, entity_a: GeoEntity, entity_b: GeoEntity, description: str) -> str:
        """
        Construct the full prompt for the LLM using the entity data and the
        vernacular description. The prompt includes the reasoning template
        and the entity details.
        """
        # Validate geometry types
        if entity_a.geometry_type != "Polygon" or entity_b.geometry_type != "Polygon":
            raise ValueError("GeoReasoner only supports Polygon-to-Polygon reasoning.")

        # Build the prompt
        prompt = (
            VERNACULAR_TO_TOPOLOGY_TEMPLATE.format(
                entity_a_name=entity_a.name,
                entity_a_type=entity_a.type,
                entity_a_bbox=entity_a.bbox,
                entity_a_centroid=entity_a.centroid,
                entity_b_name=entity_b.name,
                entity_b_type=entity_b.type,
                entity_b_bbox=entity_b.bbox,
                entity_b_centroid=entity_b.centroid,
                description=description
            )
        )
        return prompt

    def _call_llm(self, prompt: str) -> str:
        """
        Send the prompt to the OpenAI LLM and return the raw response text.
        """
        response = self.client.chat.completions.create(
            model="gpt-4o-mini",
            temperature=0.0,
            max_tokens=350,
            messages=[
                {"role": "system", "content": "You are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning."},
                {"role": "user", "content": prompt}
            ]
        )
        return response.choices[0].message.content.strip()

    def reason(self, entity_a: GeoEntity, entity_b: GeoEntity, description: str) -> str:
        """
        Main entry point for topological reasoning.
        """
        prompt = self._build_prompt(entity_a, entity_b, description)
        llm_output = self._call_llm(prompt)

        # Extract the final predicate from the LLM output
        # The prompt guarantees the answer ends with "Answer: [predicate]"
        match = re.search(r"Answer:\s*(contains|within|touches|crosses|disjoint|overlaps|equals)", llm_output, re.IGNORECASE)
        if not match:
            raise RuntimeError("LLM did not produce a valid predicate.")
        predicate = match.group(1).lower()
        return predicate

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# 6. Example Usage
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Example entities (retrieved from OSM)
    desert = GeoEntity(
        name="Sahara Desert",
        type="Sahara Desert",
        geometry_type="Polygon",
        bbox=[-20.0, 10.0, 30.0, 40.0],
        centroid=[5.0, 25.0]
    )
    egypt = GeoEntity(
        name="Egypt",
        type="Egypt",
        geometry_type="Polygon",
        bbox=[20.0, 20.0, 35.0, 35.0],
        centroid=[27.5, 27.5]
    )

    # Natural language description
    description = "Sahara Desert An environmental polygon partially intersecting a national administrative polygon. Egypt"

    # Instantiate the reasoner
    reasoner = GeoReasoner(openai_api_key=os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY"))

    # Perform reasoning
    predicate = reasoner.reason(desert, egypt, description)
    print(f"Topological Predicate: {predicate}")

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# End of Geospatial Reasoning Module
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
```

**Explanation of the Reasoning Flow**

1. **Entity Validation**  
   - The `reason` method first checks that both entities are Polygons.  
   - If geometry types differ, it raises an error (the engine only handles Polygon-to-Polygon reasoning).

2. **Prompt Construction**  
   - The `_build_prompt` helper injects the OSM entity data into the `VERNACULAR_TO_TOPOLOGY_TEMPLATE`.  
   - The prompt includes the full reasoning chain and the final “Answer” line.

3. **LLM Invocation**  
   - `_call_llm` sends the prompt to the OpenAI GPT‑4o‑mini model with a deterministic temperature of 0.0.  
   - The system message reinforces the geospatial reasoning role.

  [ToT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: touches

RESULT | Expected=overlaps | Predicted=touches | WRONG | Acc=56.41%

**User Question**:  
Entity A: Tornado Alley (type: Tornado Alley, geometry: Polygon)  
Entity B: State of Oklahoma (type: State of Oklahoma, geometry: Polygon)  
Vernacular: "Tornado Alley A meteorological zone polygon intersecting heavily with a state polygon. State of Oklahoma"

**Answer**: touches
We are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning.

Vernacular-to-Topology Reference:
  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)
  CONTAINS  — "is home to", "includes", "hosts"             (A fully encloses B)
  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of",
              "surrounded by" (partial), "across from" (water boundary)
  CROSSES   — "passes through", "along", "traverses",
              "at the mouth of" / "upstream from" (full LineString)
  OVERLAPS  — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles",
              "part of population in", "county seat of" (may extend into adjacent counties)
  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from" (confirmed large distance)
  EQUALS    — "is identical to", "same boundary as", "mirrors exactly",
              "exact same extent as", "identical perimeter", "perfect mapping symmetry"
              (A and B share the exact same geometry: same interior, boundary, and exterior)

WARNING — ambiguous vernacular (read carefully):
  "county seat of" → does NOT imply within — the city may extend into adjacent counties → overlaps
  "suburb of"      → does NOT imply touches — suburb may be separated from the main city → disjoint
  "surrounded by"  → may be touches (partial encirclement), not within (full containment)
  "enclave of"     → touches (shares boundary, not interior containment)
  "along" + river/road → likely crosses (full LineString traversal), not just touches
  "same as" / "mirrors" → may be equals only if BOTH geometries are truly identical extents


Rules:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "contains" means A encloses B; "within" means A is inside B.
   — Explicitly ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A?
   — If the data shows B encloses A, the answer is "within", NOT "contains".

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "most sides", "surrounded by" with incomplete encirclement → touches.
   — "partly in", "extends into", "part of population in" → overlaps.
   — "enclave of" → touches (shared boundary, not interior containment).
   — "across from" over water → touches (water boundary is shared in GIS).
   — "county seat of" alone does NOT imply within.

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — LineString (river, road) traversing a Polygon interior → crosses.
   — LineString touching only Polygon boundary → touches.
   — "At the mouth of" / "upstream from": verify if the full LineString crosses the polygon.
   — Two Polygons partially sharing area → overlaps.

4. BOUNDING BOX CONSTRAINTS:
   — bbox overlap ≠ polygon overlap. Polygons inside overlapping bboxes may still be disjoint.
   — bbox containment ≠ within/contains. A country bbox contains almost all nearby entities.
   — Treat bbox as a clue, never as proof.

5. BBOX GAP TOLERANCE:
   — A gap of < 0.01 degrees (~1 km) between bboxes is NOT proof of disjoint.
   — Administrative boundaries often share exact edges with 0-meter gap.

6. ENTITY VALIDATION:
   — Verify the retrieved OSM entity matches the expected state/region.
   — If the entity location does not match the context, treat its geometry as unreliable.

7. FULL LINESTRING REASONING:
   — For rivers and roads, reason on the entire geometry, not just the centroid/representative point.
   — A river centroid may fall outside a city even if the river traverses it.

8. EQUALS CHECK (do before all other predicates):
   — equals requires BOTH entities to have

  [ToT] ✅ FINAL PREDICTION: equals

RESULT | Expected=overlaps | Predicted=equals | WRONG | Acc=58.14%

CRITICAL DISTINCTIONS (most confused predicates):

equals vs all others (most important to check first):
  equals   → A and B have the IDENTICAL geometry. Every point of A is a point of B and vice versa.
             Bbox sizes will be nearly identical; centroids will coincide.
             Strong signals: "same boundary", "identical perimeter", "coextensive",
             "municipal limits mirror the county boundary", "perfect mapping symmetry".
  contains / within → different size entities — one fully inside the other, NOT identical.
  overlaps → partial overlap — NEVER equals.
  If in doubt: equals is rare. Do not use it unless geometric identity is explicitly stated.

touches vs overlaps:
  touches  → A and B share ONLY their boundary. Interiors do NOT intersect.
             Example: two adjacent counties sharing a border line.
  overlaps → A and B share SOME interior area. Part of A is inside B, part is outside.
             Example: a city that partially crosses a county boundary.

touches vs crosses:
  touches  → boundary contact only, no traversal through interior.
  crosses  → A passes THROUGH B, entering AND exiting its interior.
             Requires different geometry dimensions (LineString through Polygon).
             Example: a highway that enters and exits a city polygon.

contains vs within (DIRECTION IS CRITICAL):
  contains → A is the CONTAINER. B is fully INSIDE A. Ask: "does A surround B?"
  within   → A is INSIDE B. Ask: "is A surrounded by B?"
  KEY RULE: Identify which entity is the LARGER that SURROUNDS the other.
     - YES -> contains
     - NO, B surrounds A -> within
  Do NOT default to "within" just because A is a sub-entity type or mentioned first.

disjoint vs touches:
  disjoint -> A and B share NO point at all.
  touches  -> A and B share EXACTLY their boundary edge, nothing more.
  "suburb of" alone does NOT imply touches — suburb may be disjoint from the main city.

4-STEP DISAMBIGUATION (run before concluding within, touches, or overlaps):
  Step 1: Do A and B have the EXACT SAME geometry (identical extent)?

==========================================================================================
ROW 103 | City of Denver → Denver County
Expected: equals
==========================================================================================

  [ToT] ── INPUT ──
A: City of Denver | B: Denver County | Vernacular: "A consolidated city-county where the two distinct levels map exactly 1:1."

  [ToT] ── OSM_EVIDENCE ──


  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
"""

def get_topology_reasoning(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular):
    # Retrieve OSM entities for A and B
    entity_a_osm = get_osm_entity(entity_a)
    entity_b_osm = get_osm_entity(entity_b)

    # Validate geometries
    if not entity_a_osm or not entity_b_osm:
        return "Entity geometry not found. Unable to reason."

    # Build prompt
    prompt = f"""
Entity A: {entity_a} (type: {entity_a_osm['type']}, geometry: {entity_a_osm['geometry']})
Entity B: {entity_b} (type: {entity_b_osm['type']}, geometry: {entity_b_osm['geometry']})
Vernacular: "{vernacular}"

Explore THREE different reasoning branches for the relationship between A and B using the OSM evidence.
Each branch must:
  - Check for EQUALS first: does the text describe geometrically identical / coextensive entities?
  - Check entity validation warnings before using geometry.
  - Prioritize textual qualifiers ("partly", "extends into", "enclave", "identical perimeter", etc.) over bbox signals.
  - Explicitly verify the A→B direction of the predicate.
  - Note whether reasoning is based on polygon geometry (reliable) or bbox only (use with caution).

Format exactly as:
BRANCH 1: [approach]
[reasoning]
Suggested predicate: [predicate]

BRANCH 2: [approach]
[reasoning]
Suggested predicate: [predicate]

BRANCH 3: [approach]
[reasoning]
Suggested predicate: [predicate]

Begin: 
"""
    # Call LLM
    response = call_llm(prompt)
    return response

# Example usage
entity_a = "City of Denver"
entity_b = "Denver County"
vernacular = "City of Denver A consolidated city-county where the two distinct levels map exactly 1:1. Denver County"

reasoning_output = get_topology_reasoning(entity_a, entity_b, vernacular)
print(reasoning_output)
```

**Key Points to Remember**  
- **Always ask for direction** before reasoning.  
- **Validate OSM geometries**; if missing or mismatched, rely on text.  
- **Prioritize textual qualifiers** over geometric hints.  
- **Use the 4-branch exploration** to surface all plausible predicates.  
- **End with the chosen predicate**.  

Good luck!
